Variable names map 1:1 onto NWB fields (see Step 4).\n    44→- Neural processing: spikes aligned to the **go cue**, binned with a **40 ms sliding window** and converted to **rates in Hz** (divide by bin width). The stride (3.4 ms) equals the video frame period, chosen so that neural bins align with video frames. Our task specifies **50 ms non-overlapping bins**, i.e. bw = stride = 50 ms (documented deviation required by the decoder spec).\n    45→- Neuron curation: **only classifier-'good' units** (region-specific logistic-regression classifiers described in the QC white paper) **that also have a CCF annotation** are used.\n    46→- Trial curation for the *reference analyses*: photostim, free/auto water, early-lick and no-response (ignore) trials are excluded. Our decoder must *predict* early lick and outcome (ignore/miss/hit) and *receives photostimulation as an input*, so those trials must be **kept** (documented deviation).\n    47→- No dF/F is needed (electrophysiology).\n    48→- Additional neuron filter used in the method paper analyses: neurons with mean firing rate **< 2 Hz excluded**; in this conversion this would discard 33% of good units and remove the sparse-firing population that still carries decodable information, so it is **not** applied (documented in Step 5).\n    49→\n    50→---\n    51→\n    52→## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n    53→**Status**: COMPLETE\n    54→\n    55→### Data Structure\n    56→- `/app/data/dandiset.yaml` + 28 subject folders `sub-<id>/`, 174 NWB (HDF5, NWB 2.6) files named\n    57→  `sub-<id>_ses-<YYYYMMDDTHHMMSS>_behavior+ecephys+ogen.nwb` (DANDI 000363, Chen et al. MAP dataset).\n    58→- Contents of each file (verified by `h5py.visititems`):\n    59→  - `intervals/trials` (one row per trial): `start_time`, `stop_time`, `outcome` {hit, miss, ignore},\n    60→    `early_lick` {early, no early}, `trial_instruction` {left, right}, `auto_water`, `free_water`,\n    61→    `photostim_onset` / `photostim_duration` / `photostim_power` (strings, `'N/A'` when no stim, onset given\n    62→    relative to trial start), `task` = 'audio delay', `task_protocol` = 1, `id`, `trial`, `trial_uid`.\n    63→  - `acquisition/BehavioralEvents/*`: TimeSeries with **absolute session timestamps**:\n    64→    `presample/sample/delay/go/trialend_start_times` and `_stop_times`, `left_lick_times`, `right_lick_times`,\n    65→    `photostim_start_times` (data = laser power in mW, `control` = stim site code 1/2/4/6), `photostim_stop_times`.\n    66→    `sample_start_times` has MORE entries than trials because the sample epoch is **replayed after early licks**.\n    67→  - `acquisition/BehavioralTimeSeries/Camera0_side_{Jaw,Nose,Tongue}Tracking`: DeepLabCut markers,\n    68→    `data` = (n_frames, 3) = (x, y, likelihood), `timestamps` = absolute session time, **dt = 0.0034 s (~294 Hz)**.\n    69→    Some sessions additionally contain `Camera0_side_WhiskerTracking_whisker` (20), `Camera0_side_LickPortTracking` (4)\n    70→    or a second camera `Camera3_side_*` (3). All 174 sessions have the side Jaw/Nose/Tongue markers.\n    71→  - `units` table: `spike_times` (+`spike_times_index`) with **absolute session times**, `obs_intervals`\n    72→    (= exactly the [trial start, trial stop] windows; **spikes exist only inside trials**),\n    73→    `classification` {good, unlabelled} = output of the region-specific QC classifiers, `unit_quality` {good, multi},\n    74→    `anno_name` = CCF annotation (empty string exactly for the `unlabelled` units), `electrodes` /`electrodes_index`,\n    75→    `electrode_group`, 15+ QC metrics (`presence_ratio`, `amplitude_cutoff`, `isi_violation`, `drift_metric`,\n    76→    `avg_firing_rate`, `unit_amp`, `unit_snr`, ...), `is_good_trials` (n_units x n_trials bool).\n    77→  - `general/extracellular_ephys`: one `ElectrodeGroup` per probe insertion, whose `location` attribute is a JSON\n    78→    string with the targeted `brain_regions` (e.g. 'left ALM', 'right Medulla') and stereotaxic coordinates;\n    79→    `electrodes` table with CCF coordinates `x` (ML), `y` (DV), `z` (AP) in um.\n    80→  - `general/subject/subject_id`, `session_start_time`, `identifier` (e.g. `SC015_20190207_120657_s1`).\n    81→- No `processing` module, no `stimulus` content.\n    82→\n    83→### Dataset Size (from data files)\n    84→| Statistic | Value |\n    85→|-----------|-------|\n    86→| NWB files (sessions) | 174 (173 with >=1 good unit; `SC017_20190216_162508_s4` has 0 good units) |\n    87→| Units (all clusters) | 272,227 |\n    88→| Units classified 'good' | **69,453** (25.5% of clusters) |\n    89→| Good units / session | mean 399, range 0-923 |\n    90→| Subjects | 28 |\n    91→| Sessions / subject | mean 6.2 (range 1-13) |\n    92→| Trials (total) | 94,990 |\n    93→| Trials / session | mean 546, range 264-800 |\n    94→| Probe insertions (ElectrodeGroups) | 659 |\n    95→| Outcome distribution | hit 68.7%, miss 16.5%, ignore 14.8% |\n    96→| Early lick | 11.4% |\n    97→| Instruction | right 51.5% / left 48.5% |\n    98→| Photostim trials | 18,588 (19.6%); 168/174 sessions have stim |\n    99→| Auto-water trials | 1,339 ; free-water trials 2,450 |\n   100→| CCF annotations (good units) | 293 distinct leaf structures |\n   101→| Video frame period | 0.0034 s in every session |\n   102→\n   103→### Timing facts measured from the data (critical for conversion)\n   104→| Quantity | Value |\n   105→|----------|-------|\n   106→| go cue - trial start | median 3.15 s; 1st percentile 2.19 s; min 2.11 s; **3.1% of trials < 2.5 s** |\n   107→| trial stop - go cue, hit trials | median 1.82 s, min 1.58 s (100% >= 1.5 s) |\n   108→| trial stop - go cue, ignore trials | 1.80 s (100% >= 1.5 s) |\n   109→| trial stop - go cue, **miss (error) trials** | median 0.79 s; **only 5.1% >= 1.5 s** (timeout ends the trial early) |\n   110→| go cue - sample (tone) onset | 1.85 s = 0.65 s sample + 1.2 s delay (replayed sample epochs are later) |\n   111→| photostim (when present) | starts ~1.2 s before go cue, duration 0.5 s -> ends before the go cue |\n   112→\n   113→Consequence: spike data and video frames are only defined inside [trial start, trial stop]. Requiring the full\n   114→[-2.5, +1.5] s window to be observed would delete ~95% of error (miss) trials and make the `outcome` output\n   115→nearly degenerate; unobserved bins are therefore zero-filled for neural data (no spikes recorded) and the\n   116→'not visible' class is used for the tongue (no video frames).\n   117→\n   118→---\n   119→\n   120→## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n   121→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   122→\n   123→### Expected Statistics (from papers/methods)\n   124→| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n   125→|-----------|-------|--------------|\n   126→| Good units (total) | 69,943 | \"the dataset consisted of 69,943 good units recorded across 173 behavioral sessions\" (methods.txt / datapaper) |\n   127→| Fraction of KS2 clusters kept | 25.9% | \"This corresponds to 25.9 % of clusters reported by Kilosort2.\" |\n   128→| Good units by area | ALM 8717, striatum 7664, thalamus 12808, midbrain 7495, medulla 2928 | methods.txt |\n   129→| Sessions | 173 behavioral sessions | methods.txt, datapaper Fig. 1J |\n   130→| Probe insertions | 655 (Fig 1J caption: 660 penetrations) | methods.txt / datapaper |\n   131→| Subjects | 28 mice | \"aggregated over 660 penetrations, 173 behavioral sessions, and 28 mice\"; In NWB this is the `units/classification` column.\n   146→- Alignment: reference DataJoint export stores spike times **relative to the go cue**; the reference marker alignment\n   147→  script (`align_markers.py`) also aligns video to the **go cue** over [-3, 1.5] s.\n   148→- Binning: 40 ms sliding window, 3.4 ms stride, rates in Hz (`sliding_histogram`).\n   149→- Trial selection for the reference analyses: exclude photostim, free water, early lick and ignore (no-response) trials.\n   150→- Neuron selection: classifier-good + CCF-annotated; additionally FR < 2 Hz excluded for the regression analyses.\n   151→\n   152→### Curation Steps\n   153→\n   154→**Neuron curation rules** (reference): `units/classification == 'good'` (classifier QC) AND a non-empty CCF annotation\n   155→(in NWB these two are equivalent: every unlabelled unit has an empty `anno_name`).\n   156→\n   157→**Trial curation rules** (reference): regular trials = no early lick, no auto water, no free water, no ignore,\n   158→no photostim. For this decoding task early lick / outcome are *targets* and photostim is an *input*, so only\n   159→auto-water and free-water trials (where reward delivery is not driven by the animal's choice) are dropped, plus\n   160→trials whose pre-go-cue window is not observed.\n   161→\n   162→### Decoders Trained (accuracies reported in the papers)\n   163→| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n   164→|---|---|\n   165→| Choice (lick direction) from **video** during delay epoch | AUC 0.66 +- 0.12 s.d. (n = 106 sessions) |\n   166→| Choice from video, response epoch | AUC ~0.9 (\"choice being easily decodable from video of directional licking\") |\n   167→| Choice from video, sample epoch | at chance (AUC ~0.5) |\n   168→| Single-neuron choice selectivity criterion | AUC > 0.65 |\n   169→| Neural population choice decoding (data paper, CD-based) | not given as accuracy; choice-selective activity widespread |\n   170→No paper reports accuracy for decoding outcome, early lick, or tongue position from neural activity; the neural\n   171→choice decoding in the data paper (Fig. 2) is near-perfect during the response epoch for ALM populations.\n   172→\n   173→---\n   174→\n   175→## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n   176→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   177→\n   178→### Mapping of good units, 25.9% of KS2 clusters | Agree within 0.7%; the small difference is the published-vs-archived QC list. Use `classification=='good'`. |\n   200→| Sessions | one folder per session | 174 NWB files, **173** with >=1 good unit | 173 behavioral sessions | The extra file (`SC017_20190216_162508_s4`) has 0 good units -> naturally dropped. Consistent. |\n   201→| Insertions | per-probe .mat files | 659 ElectrodeGroups | 655 (text) / 660 (Fig. 1J) | Consistent within rounding of the published numbers. |\n   202→| Per-area unit counts | region split by CCF label + hemisphere | ALM 7,885; Striatum 7,839; Thalamus 12,917; Midbrain 7,500; Medulla 2,918 (my CCF keyword mapping) | ALM 8,717; striatum 7,664; thalamus 12,808; midbrain 7,495; medulla 2,928 | Excellent agreement (<2% for 4/5 areas; ALM differs by 9% because the paper's ALM is a stereotaxic volume, mine is MOp/MOs units). divided by bin width -> **rate in Hz**, float32 | `sliding_histogram`, `process_one_area` | same rate definition as reference, bin width/stride set to 50 ms by the task spec |\n   218→| `sample_start_times` (last one before the go cue) | `input[0]` = 'time from tone onset (s)' | bin-center time minus tone onset time, seconds, float32 | new (spec) | continuous, time-varying |\n   219→| `photostim_start_times` / `photostim_stop_times` | `input[1]` = 'photostimulation on' | 1 if the bin overlaps a stim interval else 0 | `task_stimulation` in reference | binary, time-varying |\n   220→| `trials/outcome` + `trials/trial_instruction` | `output[0]` = 'lick direction choice' | ignore->'no lick'; hit->instruction; miss->opposite of instruction | `behavior_report`, `task_trial_type` | cross-checked against first lick after the go cue (99.4% agreement) |\n   221→| `trials/outcome` | `output[1]` = 'outcome' | ignore=0, miss=1, hit=2 | `behavior_report` | |\n   222→| `trials/early_lick` | `output[2]` = 'early lick' | 'no early'=0, 'early'=1 | `behavior_early_report` | |\n   223→| `Camera0_side_TongueTracking/data` (y, likelihood) | `output[3]` = 'tongue y-position' | per bin: visible frames = likelihood > 0.9; if none -> 3 ('not visible'); else mean y -> 0/1/2 using the session's 40th/60th percentiles | `align_markers.py` (marker/go-cue alignment) | percentiles computed per session over all visible bins of that session |\n   224→| `units/anno_name` + electrode CCF `x` | `brain_region_idx` | CCF leaf -> coarse group (ALM, Striatum, Thalamus, Midbrain, Medulla, Pons, Cerebellum, Hypothalamus, Hippocampus, Orbital, OtherCortex, Olfactory, CorticalSubplate, Pallidum) x hemisphere (ML >= 5700 um = left) | `helper_get_neuron_id_area` | same 14 groups and same midline as the reference code |\n   225→| `general/subject/subject_id` | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | string id per session | | 28 mice |\n   226→\n   227→### Key Decisions\n   228→1. **Neuron curation = `units/classification == 'good'`**: this column is exactly the output of the region-specific QC classifiers described in the white paper and used by the reference code (`qc_mode='classifier'`). No further firing-rate threshold is applied: the method paper's 2-Hz cut was specific to its ridge-regression encoding analysis, would remove 33% of the units, and the decoder benefits from all recorded units.\n   229→2. **Trial curation**: drop only `auto_water` and `free_water` trials (1,339 + 2,450; reward not driven by the animal's choice, and the reference `get_regular_trial_mask` also drops them). Early-lick, ignore/miss and photostim trials are **kept** because the decoder must predict early lick and outcome and receives photostim as an input; dropping them would make three of the four outputs degenerate.\n   230→3. **No coverage-based trial filtering**: spikes and video exist only inside [trial start, trial stop]. Error (miss) trials are terminated ~0.8 s after the go cue, so requiring the whole [-2.5, +1.5] s window to be observed would delete 95% of miss trials. Instead the window is cut from the **absolute** session time base, so every spike/frame that was recorded in the window (including from the neighbouring trial) is used, and the short inter-trial gaps contribute zero spikes / 'not visible'. Worst-case coverage is 66% of the window, mean ~97%.\n   231→4. **Rates in Hz** (spike count / 0.05 s), as in `sliding_histogram(rate=True)`.\n   232→5. **Choice from the trial table** (outcome x instruction) rather than from lick times: it is the same variable the reference code uses (`behavior_report`), is defined for every trial, and agrees with the first-lick-after-go-cue definition on 99.4% of trials.\n   233→6. **Tongue visibility threshold 0.9** on the DLC likelihood: the likelihood distribution is strongly bimodal (99.8% of frames are <0.1 or >0.9), so the exact threshold is irrelevant.\n   234→7. **Per-session tongue percentiles** computed over the visible bins of that session's extracted windows, exactly as the spec states ('per-session discretization').\n   235→8. **Brain regions are hemisphere-specific** ('left ALM', 'right Medulla', ...), matching the reference code's `left_/right_` area split.\n   236→9. **All 173 sessions with >=1 good unit are kept** (the 174th has no good units). No behavioral-performance session filter is applied: the DANDI release already contains the sessions the authors published, and dropping low-performance sessions would remove error trials needed for the outcome output.\n   237→\n   238→### Planned Sanity Checks\n   239→- [x] Number of good units per coarse region vs data-paper Fig. 1J numbers.\n   240→- [x] 173 sessions / 28 mice / 659 insertions vs paper (173 / 28 / 655-660).\n   241→- [x] Behavioral performance ~84%, trials/session ~476-546.\n   242→- [ ] Independent re-computation of binned rates for a random (session, trial, neuron) directly from the NWB file (`np.allclose`).\n   243→- [ ] Independent re-computation of the photostim input and the time-from-tone input for random trials.\n   244→- [ ] Independent re-computation of the tongue class for random trials, and check the class frequencies are ~40/20/40 among visible bins.\n   245→- [ ] Choice/outcome/early-lick distributions in the converted data match the raw trial-table distributions.\n   246→- [ ] Per-trial neural array shape (n_neurons, 80) and constant n_neurons within a session.\n   247→\n   248→---\n   249→\n   250→## Step 6: Script Development\n   251→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   252→\n   253→`/app/convert_data.py` implements the plan of Step 5:\n   254→\n   255→1. `process_session(path)` opens one NWB file with `h5py` and\n   256→   * selects **good units**: `units/classification == 'good'` and a non-empty `units/anno_name`\n   257→     (reference `qc_mode='classifier'` + histology requirement),\n   258→   * assigns each unit a region `'<hemisphere> <coarse CCF group>'`; hemisphere from the CCF ML\n   259→     coordinate of the unit's electrode with the reference midline `ML = 5700 um`,\n   260→   * selects trials: drops `auto_water`/`free_water` trials (reference `get_regular_trial_mask`),\n   261→     trials outside the units' `obs_intervals`, and trials with no recorded spikes at all,\n   262→   * bins spikes into **80 non-overlapping 50 ms bins** covering [go - 2.5 s, go + 1.5 s] with\n   263→     `np.searchsorted` on the absolute spike times, dividing by the bin width to obtain **Hz**\n   264→     (reference `sliding_histogram(..., rate=True)`),\n   265→   * builds the inputs (time from instruction-tone onset; photostimulation on/off per bin),\n   266→   * builds the outputs (choice, outcome, early lick, discretized tongue y).\n   267→2. `main()` runs the sessions in a `multiprocessing.Pool` (16 workers) and assembles the final dict.\n   268→3. `--show-processing` writes `processing_<session_id>.png` with: raw spike raster vs binned rates for\n   269→   the same trial, population PSTH split by choice, the two inputs, the tongue-y histogram with the\n   270→   40th/60th percentile cuts, the tongue class raster and the per-trial outputs.\n   271→\n   272→Code inefficiencies identified:\n   273→- Reading `units/spike_times` per unit from HDF5 is slow -> the whole ragged array is read once and\n   274→  sliced with the index vector.\n   275→- Binning with nested Python loops (as in the reference `sliding_histogram`) is O(n_bins x n_trials)\n   276→  per unit -> replaced by one `np.searchsorted` per unit over all bin edges (vectorised over trials).\n   277→- Averaging the 3.4 ms video frames inside each 50 ms bin -> done with cumulative sums, no loop.\n   278→\n   279→Code speedups added: single-pass HDF5 reads, `searchsorted` binning, cumsum-based video binning and\n   280→16-way multiprocessing over sessions. Result: **~0.5 s per session**.\n   281→\n   282→### Issues found while developing (and fixed)\n   283→1. **All-zero trials**: in 8 of 173 sessions the ephys recording covers only a contiguous subset of the\n   284→   behavioural trials. The units' `obs_intervals` reveal this; trials outside them are now dropped\n   285→   (`observed_trial_mask`).\n   286→2. **Last trial of a truncated recording**: one further trial can be listed in `obs_intervals` while the\n   287→   probe was already off. Trials whose extracted window contains no spikes at all are dropped.\n   288→3. Brain-region names were `np.str_`; cast to plain `str`.\n   289→\n   290→---\n   291→\n   292→## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n   293→**Status**: COMPLETE\n   294→\n   295→`python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing` ->\n   296→`/app/conversion_sample_out.txt`, `/app/sample_data.pkl`, two `processing_*.png`.\n   297→\n   298→### Sample Statistics\n   299→| Statistic | Value |\n   300→|-----------|-------|\n   301→| Sessions | 2 (SC015_20190207_120657_s1, SC015_20190208_133600_s2) |\n   302→| Neurons (total) | 834 (459 + 375) |\n   303→| Neurons / session | 417 mean |\n   304→| Subjects | 1 (440956) |\n   305→| Trials (total) | 513 (354 + 159) |\n   306→| Trials / session | 354, 159 (the second session's ephys covers only the first 160 trials) |\n   307→| T per trial | 80 bins of 50 ms |\n   308→| time_from_tone_onset range | [-0.6, 5.7] s |\n   309→| photostim_on range | [0, 1] |\n   310→| choice distribution | no lick 0.136, left 0.456, right 0.407 |\n   311→| outcome distribution | ignore 0.136, miss 0.298, hit 0.565 |\n   312→| early_lick distribution | no 0.942, yes 0.058 |\n   313→| tongue_y distribution | 0.094 / 0.047 / 0.094 / 0.766 (i.e. 40/20/40 of the visible bins) |\n   314→\n   315→### Processing Plots Review\n   316→- Raw spike raster and the binned rate image line up in time; |\n|----------|------|-------|---------|\n| `process_all_sess_parallel(bw, stride, begin_time, end_time, data_folder, save_folder, qc_mode, n_cpu)` | `Sherlock/preprocess_all_ephys.py` -> `VideoAnalysisUtils/preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py` | LOADING | Top-level entry. Called with **bw = 0.04 s, stride = 0.0034 s, begin = -3.0 s, end = +3.0 s, qc_mode = 'classifier'** |\n| `process_one_sess(...)` | `preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py` | LOADING/CURATION | Concatenates probes of a session, reads behavior/task variables, keeps only units that have **both ephys and histology**, applies the **classifier-based good-unit list** (`goodunits` QC files), splits neurons by hemisphere and coarse region |\n| `sliding_histogram(spikeTimes, begin, end, bin_width, stride, rate=True)` | `preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py` | PROCESSING | Sliding-window spike counts -> **firing rates (spikes/s)** = count / bin_width. Bin centers from begin to end |\n| `process_one_area(...)` | `preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py` | PROCESSING | Truncates spike times (already **aligned to go cue = 0**) to [begin,end], bins them, saves `fr` of shape (n_bins, n_trials, n_neurons) |\n| `helper_get_neuron_id_area(...)` | `preprocessing_DJ_2022Aug.py` | CURATION | Hemisphere assignment using CCF ML coordinate, midline **ML = 5700 um** (`>=5700` -> left); intersects with QC good-unit ids; drops units with empty CCF label |\n| `get_regular_trial_mask(ephys_data)` | `population_decoding_utils.py`, `functions_for_r2.py` | CURATION | Regular trials = no early lick, no auto water, no free water, **not** no-response, no photostimulation |\n| `align_markers_between_lims(marker_data, go_times, t_min=-3, t_max=1.5)` | `Sherlock/align_markers.py` | PROCESSING | Aligns DeepLabCut markers (nose/tongue/jaw x,y) to **go cue**, dt = 0.0034 s, takes the **last frame within each bin** |\n| `get_bad_trial_inds(...)` | `Sherlock/align_markers.py` | CURATION | Drops trials whose number of video frames does not match the trial duration (missing/extra frames) |\n| `load_session(session_path, area)` | `population_decoding_utils.py` | LOADING | Reassembles per-area pickles into a session: concatenates `fr` over neurons |\n| `nested_cross_validation(X, y, ...)` | `population_decoding_utils.py` | ANALYSIS | PCA(16) + logistic regression decoding of binary trial labels per time point |\n\n### Notes\n- The reference preprocessing operates on **MATLAB DataJoint exports** of the same dataset that is distributed here as NWB (DANDI 000363). Variable names map 1:1 onto NWB fields (see Step 4).\n- Neural processing: spikes aligned to the **go cue**, binned with a **40 ms sliding window** and converted to **rates in Hz** (divide by bin width). The stride (3.4 ms) equals the video frame period, chosen so that neural bins align with video frames. Our task specifies **50 ms non-overlapping bins**, i.e. bw = stride = 50 ms (documented deviation required by the decoder spec).\n- Neuron curation: **only classifier-'good' units** (region-specific logistic-regression classifiers described in the QC white paper) **that also have a CCF annotation** are used.\n- Trial curation for the *reference analyses*: photostim, free/auto water, early-lick and no-response (ignore) trials are excluded. Our decoder must *predict* early lick and outcome (ignore/miss/hit) and *receives photostimulation as an input*, so those trials must be **kept** (documented deviation).\n- No dF/F is needed (electrophysiology).\n- Additional neuron filter used in the method paper analyses: neurons with mean firing rate **< 2 Hz excluded**; in this conversion this would discard 33% of good units and remove the sparse-firing population that still carries decodable information, so it is **not** applied (documented in Step 5).\n\n---\n\n## Step 2: Dataset Exploration\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Data Structure\n- `/app/data/dandiset.yaml` + 28 subject folders `sub-<id>/`, 174 NWB (HDF5, NWB 2.6) files named\n  `sub-<id>_ses-<YYYYMMDDTHHMMSS>_behavior+ecephys+ogen.nwb` (DANDI 000363, Chen et al. MAP dataset).\n- Contents of each file (verified by `h5py.visititems`):\n  - `intervals/trials` (one row per trial): `start_time`, `stop_time`, `outcome` {hit, miss, ignore},\n    `early_lick` {early, no early}, `trial_instruction` {left, right}, `auto_water`, `free_water`,\n    `photostim_onset` / `photostim_duration` / `photostim_power` (strings, `'N/A'` when no stim, onset given\n    relative to trial start), `task` = 'audio delay', `task_protocol` = 1, `id`, `trial`, `trial_uid`.\n  - `acquisition/BehavioralEvents/*`: TimeSeries with **absolute session timestamps**:\n    `presample/sample/delay/go/trialend_start_times` and `_stop_times`, `left_lick_times`, `right_lick_times`,\n    `photostim_start_times` (data = laser power in mW, `control` = stim site code 1/2/4/6), `photostim_stop_times`.\n    `sample_start_times` has MORE entries than trials because the sample epoch is **replayed after early licks**.\n  - `acquisition/BehavioralTimeSeries/Camera0_side_{Jaw,Nose,Tongue}Tracking`: DeepLabCut markers,\n    `data` = (n_frames, 3) = (x, y, likelihood), `timestamps` = absolute session time, **dt = 0.0034 s (~294 Hz)**.\n    Some sessions additionally contain `Camera0_side_WhiskerTracking_whisker` (20), `Camera0_side_LickPortTracking` (4)\n    or a second camera `Camera3_side_*` (3). All 174 sessions have the side Jaw/Nose/Tongue markers.\n  - `units` table: `spike_times` (+`spike_times_index`) with **absolute session times**, `obs_intervals`\n    (= exactly the [trial start, trial stop] windows; **spikes exist only inside trials**),\n    `classification` {good, unlabelled} = output of the region-specific QC classifiers, `unit_quality` {good, multi},\n    `anno_name` = CCF annotation (empty string exactly for the `unlabelled` units), `electrodes` /`electrodes_index`,\n    `electrode_group`, 15+ QC metrics (`presence_ratio`, `amplitude_cutoff`, `isi_violation`, `drift_metric`,\n    `avg_firing_rate`, `unit_amp`, `unit_snr`, ...), `is_good_trials` (n_units x n_trials bool).\n  - `general/extracellular_ephys`: one `ElectrodeGroup` per probe insertion, whose `location` attribute is a JSON\n    string with the targeted `brain_regions` (e.g. 'left ALM', 'right Medulla') and stereotaxic coordinates;\n    `electrodes` table with CCF coordinates `x` (ML), `y` (DV), `z` (AP) in um.\n  - `general/subject/subject_id`, `session_start_time`, `identifier` (e.g. `SC015_20190207_120657_s1`).\n- No `processing` module, no `stimulus` content.\n\n### Dataset Size (from data files)\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| NWB files (sessions) | 174 (173 with >=1 good unit; `SC017_20190216_162508_s4` has 0 good units) |\n| Units (all clusters) | 272,227 |\n| Units classified 'good' | **69,453** (25.5% of clusters) |\n| Good units / session | mean 399, range 0-923 |\n| Subjects | 28 |\n| Sessions / subject | mean 6.2 (range 1-13) |\n| Trials (total) | 94,990 |\n| Trials / session | mean 546, range 264-800 |\n| Probe insertions (ElectrodeGroups) | 659 |\n| Outcome distribution | hit 68.7%, miss 16.5%, ignore 14.8% |\n| Early lick | 11.4% |\n| Instruction | right 51.5% / left 48.5% |\n| Photostim trials | 18,588 (19.6%); 168/174 sessions have stim |\n| Auto-water trials | 1,339 ; free-water trials 2,450 |\n| CCF annotations (good units) | 293 distinct leaf structures |\n| Video frame period | 0.0034 s in every session |\n\n### Timing facts measured from the data (critical for conversion)\n| Quantity | Value |\n|----------|-------|\n| go cue - trial start | median 3.15 s; 1st percentile 2.19 s; min 2.11 s; **3.1% of trials < 2.5 s** |\n| trial stop - go cue, hit trials | median 1.82 s, min 1.58 s (100% >= 1.5 s) |\n| trial stop - go cue, ignore trials | 1.80 s (100% >= 1.5 s) |\n| trial stop - go cue, **miss (error) trials** | median 0.79 s; **only 5.1% >= 1.5 s** (timeout ends the trial early) |\n| go cue - sample (tone) onset | 1.85 s = 0.65 s sample + 1.2 s delay (replayed sample epochs are later) |\n| photostim (when present) | starts ~1.2 s before go cue, duration 0.5 s -> ends before the go cue |\n\nConsequence: spike data and video frames are only defined inside [trial start, trial stop]. Requiring the full\n[-2.5, +1.5] s window to be observed would delete ~95% of error (miss) trials and make the `outcome` output\nnearly degenerate; unobserved bins are therefore zero-filled for neural data (no spikes recorded) and the\n'not visible' class is used for the tongue (no video frames).\n\n---\n\n## Step 3: Reference Text Reading\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Expected Statistics (from papers/methods)\n| Statistic | Value | Source Quote |\n|-----------|-------|--------------|\n| Good units (total) | 69,943 | \"the dataset consisted of 69,943 good units recorded across 173 behavioral sessions\" (methods.txt / datapaper) |\n| Fraction of KS2 clusters kept | 25.9% | \"This corresponds to 25.9 % of clusters reported by Kilosort2.\" |\n| Good units by area | ALM 8717, striatum 7664, thalamus 12808, midbrain 7495, medulla 2928 | methods.txt |\n| Sessions | 173 behavioral sessions | methods.txt, datapaper Fig. 1J |\n| Probe insertions | 655 (Fig 1J caption: 660 penetrations) | methods.txt / datapaper |\n| Subjects | 28 mice | \"aggregated over 660 penetrations, 173 behavioral sessions, and 28 mice\"; method paper: \"data from 28 mice\" |\n| Trials / session | mean 476, range 130-785 | \"Mice performed on average 476 (Mean; range, 130-785) trials per session\" |\n| Correct rate | 84% (range 65-99%) | \"with 84% correct rate (range, 65-99%)\" (control, non-early-lick trials) |\n| Session inclusion (data paper) | performance > 65% and >= 50 correct lick-left and lick-right trials | methods.txt |\n| Photostim trials | ~25% randomly interleaved, 17 VGAT-ChR2 mice | methods.txt |\n| Photostim effect | performance 83.2% -> 71.7% under bilateral ALM stim (93 sessions) | methods.txt |\n| Neural bin width / stride | 40 ms width, 3.4 ms stride | method paper Methods: \"binned spikes into firing rates with a bin width of 40 ms and a stride of 3.4 ms\" |\n| Video rate | 300 Hz (actual dt in files 3.4 ms) | method paper |\n| Markers | nose, tongue, jaw tracked with DeepLabCut | method paper |\n| Task epochs | sample 2 x (3 x 150 ms tones) ~0.65 s, delay 1.2 s, answer 1.5 s | methods.txt |\n| Sessions usable for video analyses | 105-106 | method paper Fig. 2 statistics (n = 105 sessions) |\n\n### Processing Details\n- Spike sorting: Kilosort2, then 15 QC metrics + **region-specific logistic-regression classifiers** trained on manual\n  Phy labels -> units labelled 'good'. In NWB this is the `units/classification` column.\n- Alignment: reference DataJoint export stores spike times **relative to the go cue**; the reference marker alignment\n  script (`align_markers.py`) also aligns video to the **go cue** over [-3, 1.5] s.\n- Binning: 40 ms sliding window, 3.4 ms stride, rates in Hz (`sliding_histogram`).\n- Trial selection for the reference analyses: exclude photostim, free water, early lick and ignore (no-response) trials.\n- Neuron selection: classifier-good + CCF-annotated; additionally FR < 2 Hz excluded for the regression analyses.\n\n### Curation Steps\n\n**Neuron curation rules** (reference): `units/classification == 'good'` (classifier QC) AND a non-empty CCF annotation\n(in NWB these two are equivalent: every unlabelled unit has an empty `anno_name`).\n\n**Trial curation rules** (reference): regular trials = no early lick, no auto water, no free water, no ignore,\nno photostim. For this decoding task early lick / outcome are *targets* and photostim is an *input*, so only\nauto-water and free-water trials (where reward delivery is not driven by the animal's choice) are dropped, plus\ntrials whose pre-go-cue window is not observed.\n\n### Decoders Trained (accuracies reported in the papers)\n| Decoded variable | Accuracy |\n|---|---|\n| Choice (lick direction) from **video** during delay epoch | AUC 0.66 +- 0.12 s.d. (n = 106 sessions) |\n| Choice from video, response epoch | AUC ~0.9 (\"choice being easily decodable from video of directional licking\") |\n| Choice from video, sample epoch | at chance (AUC ~0.5) |\n| Single-neuron choice selectivity criterion | AUC > 0.65 |\n| Neural population choice decoding (data paper, CD-based) | not given as accuracy; choice-selective activity widespread |\nNo paper reports accuracy for decoding outcome, early lick, or tongue position from neural activity; the neural\nchoice decoding in the data paper (Fig. 2) is near-perfect during the response epoch for ALM populations.\n\n---\n\n## Step 4: Check for Consistency\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Mapping of reference (DataJoint .mat) variables to NWB fields\n| Reference code variable | NWB field | Verified `free_water` | yes |\n| `behavior_report` (1 correct / 0 error / -1 no response) | `intervals/trials/outcome` (hit / miss / ignore) | yes |\n| `task_trial_type` ('l'/'r') | `sample_start_times` / `sample_stop_times` (more events than trials because of early-lick replays) | yes |\n| `task_delay_time` | `delay_start_times` / `delay_stop_times` | yes |\n| `task_stimulation` [power, type, on, off] | `trials/photostim_power`, `photostim_onset`, `photostim_duration` and `BehavioralEvents/photostim_start_times` (`control` = site code) | yes |\n| `behavior_lick_times` / |\n|-------|-----------|------------|------------|------------|\n| Good units | classifier QC list per session | 69,453 units with `classification=='good'` (25.5% of 272,227 clusters) | 69,943 good units, 25.9% of KS2 clusters | Agree within 0.7%; the small difference is the published-vs-archived QC list. Use `classification=='good'`. |\n| Sessions | one folder per session | 174 NWB files, **173** with >=1 good unit | 173 behavioral sessions | The extra file (`SC017_20190216_162508_s4`) has 0 good units -> naturally dropped. Consistent. |\n| Insertions | per-probe .mat files | 659 ElectrodeGroups | 655 (text) / 660 (Fig. 1J) | Consistent within rounding of the published numbers. |\n| Per-area unit counts | region split by CCF label + hemisphere | ALM 7,885; Striatum 7,839; Thalamus 12,917; Midbrain 7,500; Medulla 2,918 (my CCF keyword mapping) | ALM 8,717; striatum 7,664; thalamus 12,808; midbrain 7,495; medulla 2,928 | Excellent agreement (<2% for 4/5 areas; ALM differs by 9% because the paper's ALM is a stereotaxic volume, mine is MOp/MOs units). Mapping accepted. |\n| Spike alignment | spike times already relative to go cue | absolute session times; `obs_intervals` = [trial start, stop] | go-cue alignment | Subtract the go cue time per trial; identical result. |\n| Behavioral performance | - | control, non-early trials: hit/(hit+miss) = 81.0%, range 54-97% | 84% correct, range 65-99% | Same statistic to within 3%; the paper additionally applied session-selection criteria (>65% performance, >=50 correct trials of each type) that the DANDI release does not apply. |\n| Trials/session | - | 546 (all trials), 484 (non-early), range 264-800 | mean 476, range 130-785 | Consistent (the paper counts trials after removing early-lick/ignore trials in some sessions). |\n| Trial exclusions | drop early-lick, ignore, photostim, free/auto water | those trials exist and are labelled | same | Decoder spec requires early lick / outcome as targets and photostim as an input -> only auto-water and free-water trials are dropped (see Step 5). |\n| Binning | 40 ms window, 3.4 ms stride | - | same | Decoder spec requires 50 ms bins -> 50 ms non-overlapping bins (rates in Hz, same formula count/bin width). |\n\n---\n\n## Step 5: Mapping Planning\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Variable Mapping\n| Source Variable | Target Field | Transform | Reference Code Function(s) | Notes |\n|-----------------|--------------|-----------|----------------------------|-------|\n| `units/spike_times` (good units) | `neural[session][trial]` (n_neurons, 80) | absolute spike times -> histogram in 80 non-overlapping 50-ms bins spanning [go-2.5 s, go+1.5 s]; divided by bin width -> **rate in Hz**, float32 | `sliding_histogram`, `process_one_area` | same rate definition as reference, bin width/stride set to 50 ms by the task spec |\n| `sample_start_times` (last one before the go cue) | `input[0]` = 'time from tone onset (s)' | bin-center time minus tone onset time, seconds, float32 | new (spec) | continuous, time-varying |\n| `photostim_start_times` / `photostim_stop_times` | `input[1]` = 'photostimulation on' | 1 if the bin overlaps a stim interval else 0 | `task_stimulation` in reference | binary, time-varying |\n| `trials/outcome` + `trials/trial_instruction` | `output[0]` = 'lick direction choice' | ignore->'no lick'; hit->instruction; miss->opposite of instruction | `behavior_report`, `task_trial_type` | cross-checked against first lick after the go cue (99.4% agreement) |\n| `trials/outcome` | `output[1]` = 'outcome' | ignore=0, miss=1, hit=2 | `behavior_report` | |\n| `trials/early_lick` | `output[2]` = 'early lick' | 'no early'=0, 'early'=1 | `behavior_early_report` | |\n| `Camera0_side_TongueTracking/data` (y, likelihood) | `output[3]` = 'tongue y-position' | per bin: visible frames = likelihood > 0.9; if none -> 3 ('not visible'); else mean y -> 0/1/2 using the session's 40th/60th percentiles | `align_markers.py` (marker/go-cue alignment) | percentiles computed per session over all visible bins of that session |\n| `units/anno_name` + electrode CCF `x` | `brain_region_idx` | CCF leaf -> coarse group (ALM, Striatum, Thalamus, Midbrain, Medulla, Pons, Cerebellum, Hypothalamus, Hippocampus, Orbital, OtherCortex, Olfactory, CorticalSubplate, Pallidum) x hemisphere (ML >= 5700 um = left) | `helper_get_neuron_id_area` | same 14 groups and same midline as the reference code |\n| `general/subject/subject_id` | `subjects`, `subject_idx` | string id per session | | 28 mice |\n\n### Key Decisions\n1. **Neuron curation = `units/classification == 'good'`**: this column is exactly the output of the region-specific QC classifiers described in the white paper and used by the reference code (`qc_mode='classifier'`). No further firing-rate threshold is applied: the method paper's 2-Hz cut was specific to its ridge-regression encoding analysis, would remove 33% of the units, and the decoder benefits from all recorded units.\n2. **Trial curation**: drop only `auto_water` and `free_water` trials (1,339 + 2,450; reward not driven by the animal's choice, and the reference `get_regular_trial_mask` also drops them). Early-lick, ignore/miss and photostim trials are **kept** because the decoder must predict early lick and outcome and receives photostim as an input; dropping them would make three of the four outputs degenerate.\n3. **No coverage-based trial filtering**: spikes and video exist only inside [trial start, trial stop]. Error (miss) trials are terminated ~0.8 s after the go cue, so requiring the whole [-2.5, +1.5] s window to be observed would delete 95% of miss trials. Instead the window is cut from the **absolute** session time base, so every spike/frame that was recorded in the window (including from the neighbouring trial) is used, and the short inter-trial gaps contribute zero spikes / 'not visible'. Worst-case coverage is 66% of the window, mean ~97%.\n4. **Rates in Hz** (spike count / 0.05 s), as in `sliding_histogram(rate=True)`.\n5. **Choice from the trial table** (outcome x instruction) rather than from lick times: it is the same variable the reference code uses (`behavior_report`), is defined for every trial, and agrees with the first-lick-after-go-cue definition on 99.4% of trials.\n6. **Tongue visibility threshold 0.9** on the DLC likelihood: the likelihood distribution is strongly bimodal (99.8% of frames are <0.1 or >0.9), so the exact threshold is irrelevant.\n7. **Per-session tongue percentiles** computed over the visible bins of that session's extracted windows, exactly as the spec states ('per-session discretization').\n8. **Brain regions are hemisphere-specific** ('left ALM', 'right Medulla', ...), matching the reference code's `left_/right_` area split.\n9. **All 173 sessions with >=1 good unit are kept** (the 174th has no good units). No behavioral-performance session filter is applied: the DANDI release already contains the sessions the authors published, and dropping low-performance sessions would remove error trials needed for the outcome output.\n\n### Planned Sanity Checks\n- [x] Number of good units per coarse region vs data-paper Fig. 1J numbers.\n- [x] 173 sessions / 28 mice / 659 insertions vs paper (173 / 28 / 655-660).\n- [x] Behavioral performance ~84%, trials/session ~476-546.\n- [ ] Independent re-computation of binned rates for a random (session, trial, neuron) directly from the NWB file (`np.allclose`).\n- [ ] Independent re-computation of the photostim input and the time-from-tone input for random trials.\n- [ ] Independent re-computation of the tongue class for random trials, and check the class frequencies are ~40/20/40 among visible bins.\n- [ ] Choice/outcome/early-lick distributions in the converted data match the raw trial-table distributions.\n- [ ] Per-trial neural array shape (n_neurons, 80) and constant n_neurons within a session.\n\n---\n\n## Step 6: Script Development\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`/app/convert_data.py` implements the plan of Step 5:\n\n1. `process_session(path)` opens one NWB file with `h5py` and\n   * selects **good units**: `units/classification == 'good'` and a non-empty `units/anno_name`\n     (reference `qc_mode='classifier'` + histology requirement),\n   * assigns each unit a region `'<hemisphere> <coarse CCF group>'`; hemisphere from the CCF ML\n     coordinate of the unit's electrode with the reference midline `ML = 5700 um`,\n   * selects trials: drops `auto_water`/`free_water` trials (reference `get_regular_trial_mask`),\n     trials outside the units' `obs_intervals`, and trials with no recorded spikes at all,\n   * bins spikes into **80 non-overlapping 50 ms bins** covering [go - 2.5 s, go + 1.5 s] with\n     `np.searchsorted` on the absolute spike times, dividing by the bin width to obtain **Hz**\n     (reference `sliding_histogram(..., rate=True)`),\n   * builds the inputs (time from instruction-tone onset; photostimulation on/off per bin),\n   * builds the outputs (choice, outcome, early lick, discretized tongue y).\n2. `main()` runs the sessions in a `multiprocessing.Pool` (16 workers) and assembles the final dict.\n3. `--show-processing` writes `processing_<session_id>.png` with: raw spike raster vs binned rates for\n   the same trial, population PSTH split by choice, the two inputs, the tongue-y histogram with the\n   40th/60th percentile cuts, the tongue class raster and the per-trial outputs.\n\nCode inefficiencies identified:\n- Reading `units/spike_times` per unit from HDF5 is slow -> the whole ragged array is read once and\n  sliced with the index vector.\n- Binning with nested Python loops (as in the reference `sliding_histogram`) is O(n_bins x n_trials)\n  per unit -> replaced by one `np.searchsorted` per unit over all bin edges (vectorised over trials).\n- Averaging the 3.4 ms video frames inside each 50 ms bin -> done with cumulative sums, no loop.\n\nCode speedups added: single-pass HDF5 reads, `searchsorted` binning, cumsum-based video binning and\n16-way multiprocessing over sessions. Result: **~0.5 s per session**.\n\n### Issues found while developing (and fixed)\n1. **All-zero trials**: in 8 of 173 sessions the ephys recording covers only a contiguous subset of the\n   behavioural trials. The units' `obs_intervals` reveal this; trials outside them are now dropped\n   (`observed_trial_mask`).\n2. **Last trial of a truncated recording**: one further trial can be listed in `obs_intervals` while the\n   probe was already off. Trials whose extracted window contains no spikes at all are dropped.\n3. Brain-region names were `np.str_`; cast to plain `str`.\n\n---\n\n## Step 7: Sample Conversion and Validation\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n`python -u /app/convert_data.py /app/sample_data.pkl --sample --show-processing` ->\n`/app/conversion_sample_out.txt`, `/app/sample_data.pkl`, two `processing_*.png`.\n\n### Sample Statistics\n| Statistic | Value |\n|-----------|-------|\n| Sessions | 2 (SC015_20190207_120657_s1, SC015_20190208_133600_s2) |\n| Neurons (total) | 834 (459 + 375) |\n| Neurons / session | 417 mean |\n| Subjects | 1 (440956) |\n| Trials (total) | 513 (354 + 159) |\n| Trials / session | 354, 159 (the second session's ephys covers only the first 160 trials) |\n| T per trial | 80 bins of 50 ms |\n| time_from_tone_onset range | [-0.6, 5.7] s |\n| photostim_on range | [0, 1] |\n| choice distribution | no lick 0.136, left 0.456, right 0.407 |\n| outcome distribution | ignore 0.136, miss 0.298, hit 0.565 |\n| early_lick distribution | no 0.942, yes 0.058 |\n| tongue_y distribution | 0.094 / 0.047 / 0.094 / 0.766 (i.e. 40/20/40 of the visible bins) |\n\n### Processing Plots Review\n- Raw spike raster and the binned rate image line up in time; the go cue is at 0 in both.\n- The population PSTH shows the expected go-cue-locked response, differing between lick-left and\n  lick-right trials.\n- Input 0 is a straight line through the trial, crossing zero ~1.85 s before the go cue (the tone onset).\n- Input 1 is a 0.5 s block ending before the go cue, present on ~20% of trials -> matches the protocol\n  (photoinhibition of the last 0.5 s of the delay epoch on ~25% of trials).\n- The tongue-y histogram is unimodal with the 40th/60th percentile cuts inside the bulk; the tongue class\n  raster shows licking bouts only after the go cue (or before it on early-lick trials).\n- No anomalies.\n\n### Run Time Estimates\n| Speed-ups Implemented | Time Savings |\n|---|---|\n| single read of `units/spike_times` + slicing | ~20x vs per-unit HDF5 reads |\n| `searchsorted` binning instead of nested loops | ~100x vs reference `sliding_histogram` |\n| cumsum video binning | ~50x vs per-bin loops |\n| 16-process pool | ~10x wall clock |\n\n| Step | Time / Session | Estimated Total Time |\n|---|---|---|\n| load + bin neural | ~0.35 s | ~60 s serial |\n| inputs + outputs (incl. video) | ~0.1 s | ~20 s serial |\n| whole session | 0.5 s (largest sessions ~3 s) | **< 2 min with 16 workers**, plus ~2-4 min to write the ~12 GB pickle |\n\n---\n\n## Step 8: Sample Decoder Training\n**Status**: COMPLETE\n\n### Format Validation\n- Errors: None\n- Warnings: None (`Data format is valid, no errors or warnings.`)\n\n### Decoder Results (Sample, 2 sessions / 513 trials)\n| Output | Training Balanced Acc | Validation Balanced Acc | Chance |\n|--------|-------------|--------|--------|\n| choice | 0.738 | 0.620 | 0.333 |\n| outcome | 0.765 | 0.645 | 0.333 |\n| early_lick | 0.832 | 0.733 | 0.500 |\n| tongue_y_position | 0.691 | 0.601 | 0.250 |\n\nTraining loss decreased monotonically from 7.5 to 0.57 over 200 epochs (test loss 1.05). Bins are classified as: 0 (< 40th), 1 (40th-60th, inclusive on both bounds), 2 (> 60th), 3 (not visible).\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_tongue(mean_y, visible):\n    cls = np.full(mean_y.shape, 3, dtype=np.int64)\n    vals = mean_y[visible]\n    if vals.size == 0:\n        return cls\n    p40, p60 = np.percentile(vals, [40, 60])\n    cls[visible & (mean_y < p40)] = 0\n    cls[visible & (mean_y >= p40) & (mean_y <= p60)] = 1\n    cls[visible & (mean_y > p60)] = 2\n    return cls\n```\n\niii. Multiprocessing (16 workers) is used to process sessions in parallel.\n\nii.\n```python\nfiles = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(DATA_DIR, 'sub-*', '*.nwb')))\n# ...\nwith Pool(min(args.nproc, len(files))) as pool:\n    results = pool.map(_worker, list(zip(files, plot_flags)), chunksize=1)\n```\n\n```python\nwith h5py.File(path, 'r') as f:\n    sess_id = f['identifier'][()].decode()\n    subject = f['general/subject/subject_id'][()].decode()\n    # ...\n    t = f['intervals/trials']\n    trial_start = t['start_time'][:]\n```\n\niii. The AI chose `h5py` instead of `pynwb` for performance. The CONVERSION_NOTES document that 174 NWB files were found across 28 subject folders, matching the dandiset metadata.\n\n## 1-b. How are the data split into subjects?\n\ni. Each NWB file's `general/subject/subject_id` provides the numeric subject ID. The AI's CONVERSION_NOTES confirm 28 subjects matching the paper's count.\n\n## 1-c. How are the data split into sessions?\n\ni. One NWB file equals one session. Session identity is `f['identifier']`, e.g. `SC015_20190207_120657_s1`. Sorted file paths give deterministic ordering.\n\nii.\n```python\nfiles = sorted(glob.glob(os.path.join(DATA_DIR, 'sub-*', '*.nwb')))\nsess_id = f['identifier'][()].decode()\n```\n\niii. The AI documented 174 NWB files, 173 with at least 1 good unit, matching the paper's 173 sessions.\n\n## 1-d. How are the data split into trials?\n\ni. The AI treats a mismatch between go cues and trials as grounds for skipping the session, though no such mismatch occurs in the data.\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Trials are filtered by three criteria: (1) `auto_water` or `free_water` trials are removed, (2) trials outside the units' `obs_intervals` are removed (checked across ALL good units), (3) trials with zero total spikes in the extracted window are removed. Sessions with fewer than 2 surviving trials are dropped.\n\nii.\n```python\nkeep = ~(auto_water | free_water)\nkeep &= np.isfinite(go_all)\nkeep &= observed_trial_mask(u, unit_ids, trial_start, trial_stop)\n# ...\nhas_spikes = fr.sum(axis=(0, 2)) > 0\nif not has_spikes.all():\n    fr = fr[:, has_spikes, :]\n    trial_idx = trial_idx[has_spikes]\n```\n\niii. The AI justifies removing auto/free water trials because reward delivery is not driven by the animal's choice, citing the reference code's `get_regular_trial_mask`. Early-lick, ignore, and photostim trials are kept because they are decoder targets or inputs. The zero-spike check handles edge cases at the end of truncated recordings.\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. Neural data is derived from `units/spike_times` (the sorted spike times for each unit), filtered by `units/classification` and `units/anno_name`. Go cue times from `BehavioralEvents/go_start_times` provide bin edge anchors.\n\nii.\n```python\nspikes = u['spike_times'][:]\ns0, s1 = _spike_slices(u['spike_times_index'][:])\nfr = bin_spikes(spikes, s0, s1, unit_ids, edges_abs)\n```\n\niii. The AI notes that `spike_times` is the only neural representation in the NWB files.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Spike times are binned into 80 non-overlapping 50 ms bins spanning [-2.5, +1.5] s relative to the go cue. Spike counts per bin are computed via `np.searchsorted` on flattened bin edges, then divided by bin width (0.05 s) to get firing rates in Hz. No smoothing, normalization, or baseline subtraction is applied.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef bin_spikes(spike_times, starts, stops, unit_ids, edges_abs):\n    ntrials = edges_abs.shape[0]\n    flat = edges_abs.ravel()\n    out = np.empty((len(unit_ids), ntrials, NBINS), dtype=np.float32)\n    for i, k in enumerate(unit_ids):\n        sp = spike_times[starts[k]:stops[k]]\n        idx = np.searchsorted(sp, flat).reshape(ntrials, NBINS + 1)\n        out[i] = np.diff(idx, axis=1) / BIN_SIZE\n    return out\n```\n\niii. The AI documents this matches the reference code's `sliding_histogram(..., rate=True)` formula of count/bin_width, with the bin size changed to 50 ms as required by the decoder specification.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Only units with `classification == 'good'` AND a non-empty `anno_name` (CCF annotation) are kept. Sessions with no such units are dropped. No firing rate threshold is applied.\n\nii.\n```python\nclassification = u['classification'][:]\nanno = _decode(u['anno_name'][:])\ngood = (classification == b'good')\ngood &= np.array([a.strip() != '' for a in anno])\nunit_ids = np.where(good)[0]\nif len(unit_ids) == 0:\n    return None\n```\n\niii. The AI justifies the CCF annotation requirement by noting the reference code (`helper_get_neuron_id_area`) also requires histology. The AI explicitly does NOT apply the 2 Hz firing rate filter from the method paper, arguing it was specific to encoding analyses and would remove 33% of good units.\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Spike times are already on the same session-absolute clock, so no additional alignment is needed.\n\nii.\n```python\ngo = go_all[trial_idx]\nedges_abs = go[:, None] + BIN_EDGES[None, :]\n# ...\nidx = np.searchsorted(sp, flat).reshape(ntrials, NBINS + 1)\n```\n\niii. The AI notes all NWB timestamps share the same session-absolute clock, so alignment only requires knowing the go cue time.\n\n## 2-e. What is the temporal resolution (time bin size) of the converted data? Is any temporal rebinning applied?\n\ni. 50 ms non-overlapping bins, 80 bins total spanning [-2.5, +1.5] s relative to the go cue. Bin edges are defined once as offsets.\n\nii.\n```python\nOFF_START = -2.5\nOFF_END = 1.5\nBIN_SIZE = 0.05\nNBINS = int(round((OFF_END - OFF_START) / BIN_SIZE))     # 80\nBIN_EDGES = OFF_START + BIN_SIZE * np.arange(NBINS + 1)\nBIN_CENTERS = BIN_EDGES[:-1] + BIN_SIZE / 2.0\n```\n\niii. The AI documents this as a deviation from the reference code's 40 ms window / 3.4 ms stride, required by the decoder specification.\n\n## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds* derived from?\n\ni. From `sample_start_times` timestamps in BehavioralEvents, along with `trial_start` times and go cue times. The last sample start before the go cue within each trial is selected as the tone onset.\n\nii.\n```python\nsample_start = f['acquisition/BehavioralEvents/sample_start_times/timestamps'][:] \\\n    if 'sample_start_times' in f['acquisition/BehavioralEvents'] else np.array([])\ntone = tone_onset_times(trial_start, go_all, sample_start)[trial_idx]\n```\n\niii. The AI explains that early licks replay the sample epoch, so there can be multiple `sample_start_times` per trial. The last one before the go cue is the instruction tone the animal responded to.\n\n## 3-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds*?\n\ni. For each trial, the absolute bin centers (go cue + relative bin centers) minus the tone onset time gives time from tone onset in seconds. A fallback of 1.85 s (0.65 s sample + 1.2 s delay) before the go cue is used if no sample_start is found.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef tone_onset_times(trial_start, go, sample_start):\n    ntrials = len(go)\n    tone = np.full(ntrials, np.nan)\n    if len(sample_start):\n        idx = np.searchsorted(trial_start, sample_start, side='right') - 1\n        for i, s in zip(idx, sample_start):\n            if 0 <= i < ntrials and s < go[i]:\n                if np.isnan(tone[i]) or s > tone[i]:\n                    tone[i] = s\n    missing = np.isnan(tone)\n    if missing.any():\n        tone[missing] = go[missing] - DEFAULT_SAMPLE_TO_GO\n    return tone\n```\n\n```python\ntime_from_tone = (centers_abs - tone[:, None]).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. The AI's CONVERSION_NOTES state that the fallback was never triggered.\n\n## 3-c. How is `input` *Time from tone onset in seconds* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Both the neural data and this input share the same bin grid (80 bins of 50 ms centered on the go cue). Falls back to `photostim_onset` and `photostim_duration` in the trials table (relative to trial start, stored as strings).\n\nii.\n```python\ndef photostim_intervals(f, trial_start, go):\n    be = f['acquisition/BehavioralEvents']\n    if 'photostim_start_times' in be and len(be['photostim_start_times/timestamps']) > 0:\n        on = be['photostim_start_times/timestamps'][:]\n        off = be['photostim_stop_times/timestamps'][:]\n        n = min(len(on), len(off))\n        return np.stack([on[:n], off[:n]], axis=1)\n    # fallback: reconstruct from the trials table\n    t = f['intervals/trials']\n    onset = _decode(t['photostim_onset'][:])\n    dur = _decode(t['photostim_duration'][:])\n    iv = []\n    for i in range(len(trial_start)):\n        if onset[i] != 'N/A' and dur[i] != 'N/A':\n            a = trial_start[i] + float(onset[i])\n            iv.append([a, a + float(dur[i])])\n    return np.array(iv).reshape(-1, 2)\n```\n\niii. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Photostimulation*?\n\ni. A bin is set to 1.0 if the stimulation interval overlaps the bin (i.e., stim start < bin right edge AND stim end > bin left edge), and 0.0 otherwise. This is an overlap test on the bin edges, not a center-in-interval test.\n\nii.\n```python\nstim_iv = photostim_intervals(f, trial_start, go_all)\nphotostim = np.zeros((len(trial_idx), NBINS), dtype=np.float32)\nif len(stim_iv):\n    for a, b in stim_iv:\n        photostim[(edges_abs[:, 1:] > a) & (edges_abs[:, :-1] < b)] = 1.0\n```\n\niii. The overlap test means that a bin is \"on\" if any part of the stimulation falls within the bin, not just its center.\n\n## 4-c. How is `input` *Photostimulation* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Frames with likelihood <= 0.9 are marked not visible. For each 50 ms bin, visible frames are averaged using cumulative sums. Per-session 40th/60th percentiles are computed over all visible bin means across the trial windows, giving class boundaries.\n\nii.\n```python\nTONGUE_LIKELIHOOD_THRESHOLD = 0.9\n\nvisible = (lik > TONGUE_LIKELIHOOD_THRESHOLD) & np.isfinite(y)\nyv = np.where(visible, y, 0.0)\ncs_y = np.concatenate([[0.0], np.cumsum(yv)])\ncs_n = np.concatenate([[0], np.cumsum(visible.astype(np.int64))])\nidx = np.searchsorted(ts, edges_abs)\nlo, hi = idx[:, :-1], idx[:, 1:]\nnvis = cs_n[hi] - cs_n[lo]\nsumy = cs_y[hi] - cs_y[lo]\nmean_y = np.where(nvis > 0, sumy / np.maximum(nvis, 1), np.nan)\n```\n\niii. The AI chose 0.9 as the DLC likelihood threshold, noting the distribution is strongly bimodal (99.8% of frames below 0.1 or above 0.9), so the exact value is irrelevant.\n\n## 8-c. How is `output` *Tongue y-position* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Per-session percentiles (40th, 60th) are computed over all visible bin means across the extracted trial windows. Bins are classified as: 0 (< 40th), 1 (40th-60th, inclusive on both bounds), 2 (> 60th), 3 (not visible).\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_tongue(mean_y, visible):\n    cls = np.full(mean_y.shape, 3, dtype=np.int64)\n    vals = mean_y[visible]\n    if vals.size == 0:\n        return cls\n    p40, p60 = np.percentile(vals, [40, 60])\n    cls[visible & (mean_y < p40)] = 0\n    cls[visible & (mean_y >= p40) & (mean_y <= p60)] = 1\n    cls[visible & (mean_y > p60)] = 2\n    return cls\n```\n\niii. The percentiles are computed over the visible bins of only the trial windows (not the entire session). The class balance among visible bins is reported as 40/20/40, matching the expected 40th/60th split.\n\n## 8-d. How is `output` *Tongue y-position* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni.
